# Intern

*Your workflow, cloned.*

Intern watches you work, learns your actions, and over time handles GUI tasks
the way you would: by looking at the screen, moving the mouse, and pressing
keys. No file-system shortcuts, no app-specific scripting — only what a human
operator could do.

**The vision:** a general agent that learns a task *from a user's
demonstrations* — how *they* solve it — builds a workflow from those learned
actions, and executes it. The car-insurance form is the current **vertical
slice** that proves the loop end to end; everything else generalizes outward
from it.

This document is for developers working on Intern itself.

## Current Status

> 🌲 **Task Tree** — a 3D node-graph progress map grown from the [Task List](#task-list)
> below: a hub node per phase, a small node per task, summit at 100%:
> `treetask/index.html`. Keep it synced: whenever a checkbox here flips, flip the
> matching `done` flag in that file's `PHASES` data. See [Task List](#task-list) for
> the sync convention.

**The core loop works end-to-end on the vertical slice.** Proven this far:

- **Navigation cloning confirmed** — the transformer reproduces the user's
  demonstrated field order. Trained on top-down → navigates top-down (74% exact);
  trained on bottom-up → navigates bottom-up (93% exact). Same architecture,
  opposite orders, each learned its own → **not hardcoded, genuinely cloning.**
- **End-to-end fill + submit** — live, transformer-driven: fills every field in
  the learned order (100% value accuracy via LLM), **clicks Submit on its own**
  (learned via tail-oversampling, not a hardcoded rule), advances to the next
  record.
- **Division holds:** transformer = WHERE (which element) + WHAT (click vs type);
  LLM = the value.

**Honest gaps remaining on the slice (2026-06-14):** the model now **navigates all 8
tabs and fills deep tabs** (Drivers/History/Claims, click_acc 0.88) — but doesn't yet
*complete the task* end-to-end. Open: erratic tab-visit order, `(leave blank)` typed
literally, scroll not actually moving, single record only. Closing those is **the
current priority** — see [Task List → P0](#task-list). The scope-agnostic engine is
already built (foundation); finishing scope #1 builds the general muscle the other
scopes reuse.

## How It Works

```
   ┌──────────────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────────────┐
   │   Source window          │         │   Target window          │
   │   (Notepad / Excel /     │         │   (wxPython form / web / │
   │    PDF / web / etc.)     │         │    spreadsheet / etc.)   │
   └────────────┬─────────────┘         └────────────┬─────────────┘
                │                                    │
                │ VLM screenshot                     │ UIA tree + OCR
                │ scan_tab / rescan                  │ snapshot
                ▼                                    ▼
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                Observation (state)               │
         │   focused element │ field labels │ values │ bbox │
         └────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                                  │
                                  ▼
                       ┌──────────────────────┐
                       │  LLMAgent.run() loop │
                       └──────────┬───────────┘
                                  │
              ┌───────────────────┼───────────────────┐
              ▼                   ▼                   ▼
      ┌──────────────┐   ┌────────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐
      │  TaskPlugin  │   │     LLM        │   │ Transformer  │
      │ (form-fill,  │   │ (Anthropic /   │   │  (BC policy, │
      │  scroll,     │   │  Groq / Gemini)│   │   predicts   │
      │  tab-switch) │   │  decide what   │   │   click/key) │
      └──────┬───────┘   │  to do next    │   └──────┬───────┘
             │           └────────┬───────┘          │
             └────────────────────┼──────────────────┘
                                  ▼
                       ┌──────────────────────┐
                       │   ActionExecutor     │
                       │  (pyautogui)         │
                       └──────────┬───────────┘
                                  ▼
                          click / type / scroll
                                  ▼
                          Target window updates
```

The agent re-observes after every action and loops until the plugin signals
"done" or `max_steps` is reached.

**Perception is an adapter.** Today it's the UIA tree (accurate on native
controls). The downstream pipeline consumes a semantic element list
(`bbox, role, name, value, states, …`), so the perception source is swappable —
UIA now, a vision/VLM adapter later — without touching the learning layer. See
[Big Three #1](#task-list).

## Behavioral Cloning Process

The full loop for teaching Intern a task from human demonstrations.

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  TRAINING PIPELINE                                          │
│                                                             │
│   STEP 1 — RECORD                                           │
│     python app/main.py   (GUI recorder)  or record_trace.py │
│     Human demos the task (target form + Notepad source)     │
│                      ↓                                      │
│   STEP 2 — CLEAN                                            │
│     python scripts/clean_demos.py <src> <dst>              │
│     Drops dropdown-SELECTION clicks (value-picks that land   │
│     on the field under an open dropdown), off-form-window    │
│     junk, pane clicks, consecutive dupes.                    │
│                      ↓                                      │
│   STEP 2b — (optional) OVERSAMPLE THE FINISH                │
│     python scripts/oversample_tails.py <clean> <dst>       │
│     Copies each cycle's tail (… → Submit) K× so the rare    │
│     "form complete → Submit" transition is well-represented │
│     → model LEARNS to submit (no hardcoded completion rule). │
│                      ↓                                      │
│   STEP 3 — TRAIN                                            │
│     python scripts/train.py --trace_dir <dir> --epochs 80 \ │
│       --d_model 128 --num_layers 4 --dim_feedforward 256    │
│     Trains TransformerAgentNetwork. Best checkpoint on       │
│     (val_acc + click_acc).                                   │
│                                                             │
│  Goal: imprint human demo behavior into model.pt            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                        ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  INFERENCE + EVALUATION                                     │
│                                                             │
│   STEP 4 — RUN & EVALUATE                                   │
│     python run_task.py                                      │
│     Transformer: WHERE to click + WHAT action (click/type)  │
│     LLM (LM Studio): supplies the value when action = type  │
│                                                             │
│   Offline clone check (no live form):                       │
│     python scripts/test_clone.py <session_dir>              │
│     Compares model's click_elem_idx to the user's clicked    │
│     element per frame → exact% + offset distribution.        │
│                                                             │
│   Auto-runs after a live run:                               │
│     eval_metrics.py  → TCR, field/value accuracy            │
│     bc_fidelity.py   → BC score vs gold standard            │
│     rule_extractor.py→ infers task ruleset from trace       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                        ↓
       (poor metrics → clean/record more → retrain → repeat;
        DAgger: on a failure the agent watches ~4s for the
        user to do the right action and saves it as a trace)
```

**Division of responsibility:**
- **Transformer** — *where* to click (which element) **and** *what* action
  (click vs type), learned from demos.
- **LLM** — *what* value to type (from source data). Reserved for the part that
  needs understanding, not clean lookups.

### Key features that make navigation learnable
- **`is_filled`** (per element) — does this field currently hold a value, read
  straight from the observation. Without it the model only saw field *labels* and
  was blind to which fields were done → it looped. This is *perception*, not a
  hand-tracked progress signal.
- **`is_focused`** — which element has keyboard focus.
- **Action-space collapse → {click, type}** — junk classes (stray `drag`,
  `backspace` hotkeys) were dropped/remapped so the action-type head stopped
  collapsing onto classes it could never predict. Action-type accuracy 50% → 80%,
  and click accuracy rose for free.

### Testing variations (does it clone, or just memorize?)
Record demos in multiple fill orders and confirm the agent reproduces each:
- **Top-down** — fields top-to-bottom (baseline). ✅ confirmed
- **Bottom-up** — fields bottom-to-top. ✅ confirmed (93%, proves not a down-bias)
- **Random (fixed)** — a chosen non-obvious order, repeated consistently. *pending*

A clone that handles a *different* demonstrated order proves field-level learning,
not sequence memorization. `scripts/test_clone.py` reports exact% + the offset
distribution (0 = exact, +1 = next-down, −1 = next-up) so you can see the
*direction* it learned.

## Solved Problems

What's *behind* us — the mirror of the roadblocks ahead. Each was a real blocker;
each is now closed with a verification that guards against regression.

### Behavioral cloning — the core thesis
- **The transformer clones the demonstrated order, not a bias.** Top-down→74% /
  bottom-up→93% exact on each order's own data. Same architecture, opposite
  orders → genuine cloning. *(Proof: offset-distribution analysis, `test_clone.py`.)*
- **WHERE/WHAT division works.** Transformer drives element + click/type; LLM
  supplies the value. Live: 100% value accuracy when on the right field.
- **Learned the finish.** Clicks Submit on its own via tail-oversampling — no
  hardcoded end-game rule.

### Perception / data quality
- **Looping from fill-blindness.** Model re-entered filled fields because state
  embedded only labels, never values. Fixed with the `is_filled` feature + folding
  value into the embedding → click_acc 0.95→1.00.
- **Action-type collapse.** Junk classes (backspace/drag) wrecked the action head.
  Collapsed action-space to {click, type} → action-type 29/51% → 80/80%.
- **Combobox phantom fields.** Clicks while a dropdown was open landed on the field
  beneath it → recorded a phantom order. Fixed at record time (drop selection-clicks).

### Generalization foundation *(Tier 1 — in progress, two closed)*
- **✅ Form-specific hardcodes killed → injected `ScopeConfig`.** `_detect_section`,
  `_KNOWN_TABS`, `_TAB_PANE_NAMES`, the `RECORD N OF M` delimiter — all moved out of
  agent.py into a per-scope config with generic defaults. The agent is now
  **application-blind**; a new scope passes its own config with zero agent edits.
  *(Guard: `tests/test_detect_section.py` — 8/8, locks behavior + proves the default
  scope is a no-op. Live-verified: form fills + submits unchanged.)*
- **✅ Perception adapter seam.** The observer is now **injectable** (UIA / Excel /
  web plug into one slot), and a canonical element schema (`observers/schema.py`)
  defines the one language every adapter must speak. The agent **validates the
  adapter on first observe and fails LOUD** — no more silent blank-screen when an
  adapter emits a different dialect (e.g. Excel `type=cell` / value-under-`text`).
  *(Guard: `tests/test_perception_schema.py` — 8/8, conforming passes clean, Excel's
  raw dialect is flagged with actionable errors.)*

> Still open in Tier 1: generalize the data-source layer, and the scope
> abstraction (declare a scope in one place). See [Task List](#task-list).

## Quick Start

> Recording demos for Scope #2 (grade sheet → web portal) has its own
> step-by-step guide: [docs/scope2_recording_demos.md](docs/scope2_recording_demos.md).
> Several of its setup rules fail silently rather than erroring, so read it
> before the first session rather than after.

```bash
# 1. Install Python deps (Windows; UIA + pyautogui are Win-specific)
pip install -r requirements.txt

# 2. Put API keys in .env at repo root
#    GROQ_API_KEY=...        # free, used for VLM + (optionally) LLM
#    GEMINI_API_KEY=...      # free, used as VLM fallback when Groq 429s
#    ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...   # paid, best LLM reasoning quality

# 3. Open the target form
python practice_apps/car_insurance_entry/car_insurance_form_wx.py

# 4. Open the source data in Notepad
notepad data_entry_tasks/data_entry_intake.txt

# 5. Run the agent
python run_task.py
```

Configure `run_task.py` knobs at the top:

| Constant         | Default                  | Notes                                |
|------------------|--------------------------|--------------------------------------|
| `PROVIDER`       | `"lmstudio"`             | Switch to `"groq"` / `"anthropic"` for real reasoning. |
| `MAX_STEPS`      | `200`                    | Hard cap per run.                    |
| `SOURCE_WINDOW`  | `"Notepad"`              | Title fragment of the source window. |

## Repository Layout

```
app/
  main.py                    GUI recorder (Start/Stop, replay, frame counter)
components/
  agent/
    agent.py                 LLMAgent — main loop, provider abstraction, merge
    capsule.py               Per-task model routing (goal → .pt file)
    task_plugins/            Task-specific plugins (form-fill, etc.)
  data_sources/
    notepad_source.py        Win32 WM_GETTEXT + parse_records helpers
  intelligence/
    model/
      transformer.py         TransformerAgentNetwork — BC policy model
    rule_extractor.py        LLM-based task spec generator / corrector
    training/                bc / rl / continual trainers
  observers/
    ui_observer/             UIA tree walker (semantic element list)
    vlm/                     VLM screenshot → key/value extraction
  recorder/
    recorder.py              DemoRecorder (on-demand subprocess snapshots)
    correction_handler/      DAgger: watch-for-user-correction on failure
  inbox_router/              Scope #3 — Gmail triage (rules + LLM hybrid)
    router.py                 InboxRouter — poll loop, stdin/stdout protocol
    gmail_client.py            GmailClientBase / MockGmailClient / RealGmailClient
    pattern_profile.py         Passive per-sender learning from Sent folder
    rules.py                   Deterministic first pass
    llm_classifier.py          LLM fallback/tiebreaker
tasks/
  registry.json              Global capsule registry (goal → model path)
  form_filling/
    model.pt                 Trained BC checkpoint
    ruleset.md               Inferred task spec (auto-updated each session)
practice_apps/
  car_insurance_entry/       wxPython target form (Scope #1 test fixture)
  mocksite/                  8 mock-portal variants (Scope #2 test fixture,
                              coworker's RJGanzon/Intern)
data_entry_tasks/            Source intake .txt files
data/demos/                  Recorded sessions (gitignored)
scripts/
  clean_demos.py             Drop dropdown-selection / junk / dupe clicks
  oversample_tails.py        Emphasize the … → Submit finish
  test_clone.py              Offline clone check (exact% + offset)
  augment_traces.py          Dataset augmentation (jitter)
  eval_metrics.py            TCR / field / value accuracy
  bc_fidelity.py             BC fidelity score vs gold standard
  replicate.py               Duplicate a recorded session N× (terminal)
  train.py                   BC training entrypoint
  build_capsule.py           Package model + metadata into capsule
  demo_recorder.py           Human demonstration recorder
  record_trace.py            ScreenObserver launcher
  run_agent.py                Generic LLMAgent entrypoint (older, more
                              general than run_task.py — see its own note)
  run_transformer.py         Transformer-only agent runner (no LLM)
run_task.py                  Agent entrypoint (transformer + LLM) — the
                              one script kept at repo root, run most often
```

Every other root-level `.py` utility (`replicate.py`, `train.py`,
`build_capsule.py`, `demo_recorder.py`, `record_trace.py`, `run_agent.py`,
`run_transformer.py`, plus the trace/video diagnostics `analyze_video.py`,
`generate_trace.py`, `generate_two_state_trace.py`,
`generate_s1_s3_trace.py`, `inspect_diff.py`, `inspect_text_changes.py`,
`visualize_trace.py`) moved into `scripts/` 2026-08-15, direct request
("so many .py scripts [at root]... let's not do that"). `run_task.py`
stays at root — the one script actually run most often, by far.

## Components

### Agent
- **`components/agent/agent.py`** — `LLMAgent` orchestration loop, multi-provider
  LLM support (Anthropic / Groq / Gemini / LM Studio). `_merge` glues transformer
  (WHERE/WHAT) + LLM (value). `disable_auto_handlers` runs pure transformer+LLM
  with legacy heuristics gated off.
- **`components/agent/capsule.py`** — Routes goal + window title to the correct
  `.pt` checkpoint via `tasks/registry.json`.

### Transformer (BC Policy)
- **`components/intelligence/model/transformer.py`** — `TransformerAgentNetwork`.
  Causal transformer trained via behavioral cloning. Predicts: action type
  (click/type after action-space collapse), which element to click (pointer head),
  which source element holds the value (source pointer head).
- Input per element: `is_real, bbox(4), confidence, window_role, is_focused,
  ctrl_type, is_filled, text_embedding(384)` (= `ELEM_FEATURES`).
- **LayerNorm on pointer heads** — prevents bilinear Q×K divergence.

### Observers
- **UI Automation Observer** — Walks the UIA tree → semantic element list
  (`element_id, type, label, bbox, value, focused, window_role`).
- **Vision Observer / Visual Data Reader** — Screenshot → VLM → JSON of visible
  field/value pairs. The slot for the future vision *perception adapter*.
- **OCR fallback** — Tesseract over background pixels when UIA returns empty.

### Inbox Router (Scope #3)
- **`components/inbox_router/gmail_client.py`** — `GmailClientBase` (no `send()`
  method anywhere in the interface — structurally, not just by convention, this
  subsystem cannot send real email). `MockGmailClient` backs a committed fixture;
  `RealGmailClient` is the standard `google-auth-oauthlib` Desktop-app OAuth flow.
  `get_gmail_client()` picks between them based on whether
  `credentials/client_secret.json` exists — the entire Phase A → Phase B swap.
- **`components/inbox_router/pattern_profile.py`** — `PatternProfile`, built
  passively from the Sent folder correlated against recent inbox threads (no
  manual labeling), nudged incrementally on every user confirmation/override.
- **`components/inbox_router/rules.py`** — `RuleLayer`, the deterministic first
  pass (sender pattern lopsidedness, then keyword match against
  `tasks/registry.json`'s own capsule triggers).
- **`components/inbox_router/llm_classifier.py`** — `LLMClassifier`, same
  multi-provider shape as `LLMAgent` (re-implemented, not imported — same
  choice already made for Scope #2), only called when rules don't confidently
  resolve.
- **`components/inbox_router/router.py`** — `InboxRouter`, the orchestrator;
  spawned as a child process by `app/recorder_bridge.py`'s
  `start_inbox_router()`, same shape as a capsule Play run. `confirm_suggestion()`
  is the only place a Gmail draft is ever created — draft-only, never auto-send.

### Recorder & Corrections
- **`components/recorder/recorder.py`** — `DemoRecorder`. On-demand subprocess
  UIA snapshots (off the main GIL). Clicks always get a fresh snapshot (focus
  accuracy). Filters: own-GUI-window clicks, F-keys, dropdown-selection clicks.
- **`correction_handler/`** — DAgger hook: on a validation failure the agent
  opens a ~4s window, captures the user's correcting action + state, saves it as
  a trace.

### Data Sources
- **`NotepadDataSource`** — Win32 `WM_GETTEXT` + `_parse_records` multi-record
  parser + field-line lookup helpers.

### Action Executor
- pyautogui mouse/keyboard. All actions go through this — no OS-level shortcuts a
  human couldn't do.

### Rule Extractor
- **`components/intelligence/rule_extractor.py`** — `extract()` derives rules from
  a completed run; `correct()` reads spec + new demo → corrected spec.

### Chain-of-Thought (LM Studio only)
Injects a `<think>…</think>` reasoning step for local models, stripped before JSON
parsing. Not used for Anthropic (native reasoning is better). Disable by removing
the CoT lines in `_call_openai_compat()`.

## Task List

> **Sync with Task Tree:** this list is the source of truth; `treetask/index.html`
> mirrors it as a 3D node graph. Structured around the same 11 branches as the Task
> Tree — 3 Scopes (what domains) + 8 capability/process dimensions (how it works) —
> not roadmap phases (P0–P5) anymore, so a checkbox's *category* is what it's about,
> not when it was planned. Every task has a stable id (referenced in Task Tree's
> `PHASES` data) and, where real, a `requires` — a prerequisite that must be done
> before that task can start. When a checkbox here changes, or we make a choice
> about *how* to solve something, update the matching Task Tree entry in the same
> pass, not as a separate cleanup step.

**Priority: COMPLETE SCOPE #1 (the form) first — then generalize.** The
scope-agnostic engine is built. The form navigates all 8 tabs and fills deep tabs,
but doesn't yet complete a task end-to-end (tab order erratic, scroll dead, single
record). Chasing Scope #2/#3 before Scope #1 completes is premature — its
remaining gaps (multi-tab nav, multi-record, cold-start) are *general* capabilities
every scope will hit, so finishing it isn't a detour from the thesis, it's building
the muscle Excel + triage will reuse.

> **LESSON (2026-06-14):** agent guards perturb the transformer's action-history
> (hist_len=4) → can destabilize a brittle model (piled ~8 guards once, regressed
> navigation, reverted). Add/strip agent logic **ONE at a time, re-test each**. The
> durable fix for fixation/mis-prediction is **more demos**, not more guards.

---

### Scope #1 — Data Entry Form Filling *(in progress — THE current focus)*
Single-app key-value entry into the car insurance form, (mostly) linear
navigation. Perception = UIA. **Definition of done:** agent fills all 5 records,
all 8 tabs + Driver/Vehicle sections, in the demonstrated order, submits each, no
human help.

**ROADMAP to a completed Scope #1**, written 2026-08-11 per direct request for a deep, authoritative analysis (also kept as a durable cross-session memory, `project-scope1-completion-criteria`). Two independent "done" conditions apply: the functional definition above, and the thesis' 12 numbered objectives (`scripts/objectives_report.py` for a live dashboard), each with its own hard target. Root-caused to **5 underlying problems**, not twelve independent gaps:
1. **Not enough clean recorded data** — 3,987 clean steps (5 sessions) vs. the 10,000-trace target (see `scope1_rerecord_50k` above). Blocks Objectives 3/5 (learning accuracy) and 7 (scalability). **Retrained 2026-08-11** on the full updated set (fixed empty-keystroke data + 1 new session, `session_20260811_213118`): best checkpoint epoch 78/80, `val_acc=53.9%`, `val_click_acc=83.0%` — essentially flat vs. the prior 83.2% (old checkpoint backed up as `model_pre_20260811_2200_backup.pt`). Confirms this is genuinely data-volume-limited, not something one more session + a label fix was going to fix: the real lever is still closing the gap to 10,000 traces.
2. **Action-derivation bug (empty-keystroke)** — stray modifier/nav keys surviving raw capture as empty-payload "keyboard" actions. Blocked Objective 4 (transition mapping, ~45-50% measured on the 4 clean sessions, target ≥90%). **Fixed 2026-08-11** (`components/recorder/recorder.py`'s `_derive_action_from`) — 896/2,974 affected steps backfilled, transition-mapping accuracy measured at 61.8-70.9% across sessions post-fix (up from 45-50%). New recordings (via `app/main.py`) are unaffected by construction going forward.
3. **Real, structural label collisions in the form** — Driver 2/3 share identical labels, genuinely coexist in one captured state; Payment's billing address collides with Policyholder's. Blocks Objective 2 (encoding ambiguity, ~14%, target <5%). **Real failure precedent**: a closely related fix (`disambiguate_attempted`, rank-based disambiguation for a related label-collision problem) was tried 2026-08-07/08 and *regressed* accuracy (48.2% vs 50.8%) — any fix here needs a real isolated A/B test, not an assumed win.
4. **Three objectives with nothing built yet** — Objective 1 (perception) can't even be measured: confirmed directly, Tesseract OCR isn't installed on this machine (`shutil.which("tesseract")` returns `None`) — the same root cause behind the "2 pre-existing tesseract test failures" that showed up as unrelated noise in every full-suite run this whole session. Objective 6 (unseen-GUI adaptation) has zero code path for "unseen" at all — needs a genuinely new held-out test environment built from scratch. Objectives 11/12 (RPA baseline) need real, externally-collected RPA-tool measurements — `scripts/compare_baseline.py` is ready, the data isn't.
5. **Nothing has run long enough yet to measure** — Objectives 8 (agentic integration, 0.0%), 9a/9b (execution error/wasted steps, no data), and `scope1_speed` (no baseline). Root-caused directly 2026-08-11 from real logs, not assumed: of the 15 most recent live runs, only **one** got past step 0 (44 steps) — the other 14 show "Run ended — 0 steps," manually interrupted almost immediately. Not a regression — genuinely zero real data points behind these three right now.

**Ordered plan**: (1) install Tesseract, run `perception_eval.py --live` — unlocks Objective 1, zero dependency on anything else; (2) fix the empty-keystroke recorder bug before recording more; (3) record ~7,000 more clean steps toward the 10,000 target, auditing each session the same way that already caught 25 unusable ones across two audits — the actual critical path; (4) in parallel with (3), run a real isolated A/B test on a section-context disambiguation signal for Objective 2; (5) let at least one live run play out to completion without interrupting it early — unlocks real measurement of Objectives 8/9/`scope1_speed`, can be tried now, doesn't need to wait for more data; (6) once a checkpoint clears ~90% click accuracy, `scope1_record_advance_5`/`scope1_per_record_refresh` become realistic to actually test; (7) build the unseen-GUI held-out test (Objective 6) only after the model works reliably on the known environment; (8) collect real RPA-tool baseline numbers (Objectives 11/12) anytime — entirely external, not on Intern's own critical path.

**PRIORITY RECALIBRATED, 2026-08-14, direct decision from the user** ("We spent too much on Scope #1... as long as we have a proper run, we're good. We just need to make this an end-to-end"). Real, deliberate scope-narrowing, not abandoned rigor: Scope #1's bar for moving on is now the functional definition-of-done at the top of this section (one clean multi-record run, all 8 tabs, submits, no human help) — not grinding every one of the 12 numbered thesis objectives to its exact target first. `scope2_architecture_integration` (Scope #2 section below) was already gated behind exactly this (`requires: scope1_record_advance_5, scope1_e2e_metric`), so this decision accelerates the existing plan's own sequencing rather than contradicting it.

**Where this leaves things, honestly**: the gate has NOT been cleared yet. Tonight's whole run of fixes (`execution_filled_combobox_reclick_guard`'s TENTH ROUND, `execution_type_path_focus_via_uia`, `execution_combobox_dropdown_arrow_target`, `scope1_speed`'s adaptive settle-wait) were all aimed specifically at making one clean run more likely to succeed, but none have been verified together, live, in a single pass — that's the one piece that needs the user to actually run it, per this project's own standing rule. Once a run clears the functional bar, Scope #2 work starts for real; steps (3)/(4)/(7)/(8) above are deprioritized, not abandoned, in favor of that.

**Four questions that define "actually usable," direct framing from the user 2026-08-13** (kept verbatim as the north star for this scope, restated below in product terms rather than academic objective numbers — same underlying metrics, same targets):
1. **Can it complete the task reliably?** → `scope1_e2e_metric`/`execution_agentic_integration_85`. Target ≥85%.
2. **Is it fast enough to actually be a productive tool** (not slower than the person it's automating for)? → `scope1_speed`. No fixed target number yet, but the bar is implicit: faster than doing it by hand.
3. **Minimal wasted steps across the whole workflow?** → `execution_error_rate_10`. Target ≤20% wasted-step rate.
4. **High enough completion rate and accuracy to trust it?** → `learning_action_accuracy_90` (type/click/value accuracy) feeding into #1.

**First real numbers behind all four, from the same live run** (`run_task_20260813_005301.log`, 116 steps, user-interrupted, the new semantic-mode checkpoint's first live test): (1) completion **29.5%** (52/176 fields); (2) speed **2.02s/step** (~30 steps/min) — root-caused to `LLM Dependency` at **75%** (target <5%), a hardcoded `_HIGH_CONF = 1.01` gate in `agent.py` that never lets the transformer decide WHAT alone, because its action-TYPE head historically collapsed to one class; **identified, not yet fixed** — the next real lever once accuracy work settles, see `execution_llm_call_cost_reduction` below; (3) wasted-step rate **23.3%** (target ≤20%, one specific cause — the `Renewal Policy` checkbox loop — already found and fixed same night, see `execution_filled_combobox_reclick_guard`'s TENTH ROUND); (4) value accuracy **78.7%**, live click accuracy 100% (9/9, too small a sample to trust on its own), action-TYPE accuracy still open — the backspace-label fix tested to address it (`learning_semantic_action_space_port`) came back negative (51.8%→46.1%), not adopted. None of these four numbers were available anywhere in this file before this run — every previous "no data yet" note on `scope1_speed`/`execution_agentic_integration_85`/`execution_error_rate_10` predates having a live run survive past a few steps.
- [x] `execution_llm_call_cost_reduction` — **SUPERSEDED, same night, before implementation started — not a real bottleneck.** Diagnosed 2026-08-13 from `LLM Dependency = 75%`; before acting on it, checked what that metric actually measures against the real log. `grep -c "LLM call skipped"` → 124, `grep -c "LLM prompt size"` (only logged immediately before a real API call fires) → 0 — zero real LLM network calls happened in the entire run analyzed. `LLM Dependency` tracks which CODE PATH decided a value (the LLM-merge path vs. the transformer alone), not whether a network round-trip occurred — `agent.py`'s direct-lookup fast path was already skipping the real call essentially 100% of the time. Closed, not implemented — the real bottleneck (`STEP_DELAY`, fixed and unconditional, ~half of total step time) was found and fixed instead; see `scope1_speed`'s own entry for the correction and the real fix (`_adaptive_settle_wait`).

- [ ] `scope1_tab_order` — Tab-visit order: model jumps/revisits tabs (skips Policyholder/Vehicle/Coverage). Fix via cleaner demos (consistent left-to-right order), NOT a hardcoded order. **Concrete live evidence, 2026-08-07**: after 3 rounds chasing what looked like a code bug in `execution_stuck_loop_wrong_tab_field` (pane-detection timing), a 5th live re-test caught the SAME visible symptom (Policy -> Vehicle, Policyholder skipped) with a completely different, unambiguous cause — no pane-lookup failure, no fallback path at all: `[OPT2] TRANSFORMER navigates -> click @ (1074,134) ptr_conf=0.38` landed directly on the Vehicle tab button while still on Policy (5/13 fields filled). This is exactly this task's own symptom, this time proven to be the model's own prediction, not code. Reinforces: this needs training-data fixes (tab-order consistency in demos), not more agent.py patches. Compounds with the still-unresolved `execution_stuck_loop_wrong_tab_field` code bug — both produce the identical visible symptom (Policy -> Vehicle, no Policyholder), so don't assume one without checking the log for which mechanism actually fired.

  **Re-investigated 2026-08-11, direct request ("Fix tab-visit order but make sure its not deterministic and its the model doing it").** Confirmed the architecture directly rather than re-guess: `components/intelligence/model/transformer.py`'s `TransformerAgentNetwork` has no separate "which tab" prediction head at all — `click_idx`/`click_conf` (the same generic click-position pointer used for every field) is the entire mechanism; a tab-strip button is just another element in the softmax. This is exactly what `scope1_tab_targeting` and this task's own description already said, now confirmed by reading the actual model code rather than trusting the prior description. There is no code-level lever available that wouldn't be the "hardcoded order" the user explicitly ruled out — the only real levers are (a) more/cleaner training data (the one thing with a clean track record: val_click_acc went 49.1%→50.8%→54.8%→60.4%/61.0%→83.2% purely by adding real recording sessions) and (b) the QA tooling that measures whether new recordings actually cover all 8 tabs, which turned out to have real bugs of its own — see below and `execution_bc_fidelity_tab_coverage_blind_spot`.
- [x] `scope1_leave_blank_bug` — **Fixed 2026-08-10.** `(leave blank)` could get typed literally into a field. Investigated before fixing: `_lookup_field`'s own local `_is_blank` helper was already correct (prefix match, immune to this bug) — but audited every other skip-check in `agent.py` and found **six separate call sites** each carrying their own hardcoded `{"(none)", "none", "(leave blank)", "n/a", ...}` set, checked via `value.lower().strip("()")` on the value but never applied to the set itself — so a stripped `"leave blank"` could never match an unstripped `"(leave blank)"` set entry, and the skip silently failed. Fixed by promoting the one correct implementation to a module-level `_is_leave_blank_value()` and pointing all 8 call sites at it (the 6 broken ones, plus 2 that already worked via a different strip-both-sides pattern, plus `_lookup_field`'s own copy) — single source of truth instead of eight copies that could drift. Where a site also skipped on an unrelated reason (`"yes (check)"`, `"no"` — checkbox handling, not a blank marker), that check was kept separate rather than folded in. Regression tests: `tests/test_leave_blank_value_shared_check.py` (9 new, including a direct reproduction of the old broken pattern failing where the new one succeeds). Full suite 483 passed / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures. Not yet re-verified live.
- [x] `scope1_checkbox_coldstart` — Checkbox cold-start / first-click: reliable first action from a blank screen (Renewal checkbox handling).

  **Investigated 2026-08-11: found this was already fixed, just never confirmed/marked done.** No code exists specifically for "Renewal" — the item describes a symptom, not a distinct unfixed mechanism. Traced the real historical failure directly: `logs/run_task_20260807_213014.log` shows a genuine repeating loop — `Checkbox 'Renewal Policy' already checked — Tab instead.` fired 10+ times, roughly every 9-10 seconds, with the transformer's own navigate-pointer drifting back to the checkbox's screen position in between (a 4-position oscillation: two other fields, then back to the checkbox, repeat). The guard already caught it safely each time (no re-toggle, no data corruption — the log shows the guard firing *before* a physical click, not after), but did nothing to stop the wasted step-cycles themselves.

  **That's exactly the residual `execution_filled_combobox_reclick_guard`'s fifth round (same night, 2026-08-07) already named explicitly**: "nothing stopped the wasted click before it happened" — and that round's fix (extending the reclick guard to already-attempted checkboxes) landed the same session, later that night, after this specific log was captured.

  **Verified directly against real, current data that it holds**: checked all 26 of today's (2026-08-11) `run_task_*.log` files — zero occurrences of "already checked" anywhere. The 44-step run (`run_task_20260811_152205.log`) shows Policy Number filled correctly at Step 1 (no cold-start issue at all) and `Renewal Policy` checked cleanly via `BM_SETCHECK` later in the same run, no oscillation. No code change needed here — this was confirmed already resolved by prior work, and the Task Tree/DEVELOPERS.md entries were simply never updated to reflect it.

  **Investigated 2026-08-11: the general mechanism already works correctly.** The open→poll→match→select (or Escape-and-retry) logic in `agent.py` (~line 4150+) is genuinely mature — already-open-dropdown detection, an 8-try/0.4s poll for slow renders (widened and reverted once already based on real evidence), three-tier option matching (exact/prefix-fuzzy/token-containment), and a keyboard fallback plus 2-strike escalation (`_combobox_dropdown_fail_counts`) when a dropdown never renders at all. This wasn't a naive first-pass implementation needing a fix — it already reflects several real, live-verified rounds of work.

  **Verified directly against today's real logs**: 4 comboboxes filled cleanly this session with zero retries — `Transmission`, `Education Level`, `Marital Status`, `Policy Term` — each a single open→select, no Escape, no wasted cycle. The only failures logged today are specifically `Policy Status` and `Policy Type` — **the already-separately-tracked dropdown-never-renders issue** (`execution_combobox_never_opens_keyboard_fallback`), confirmed multiple times earlier this session as a genuinely different problem (those two controls' dropdowns don't render at all, not a retry-timing issue). Conflating the two would misdirect any future fix attempt at the wrong mechanism. No code change made here — the retry timing itself is already working; the real open gap is specifically Policy Status/Policy Type's rendering, tracked separately.
- [ ] `scope1_record_advance_5` — Record advance ×5. **Correction 2026-08-12**: this entry's prior "proven 1→2" claim did not match the code — directly grepped every assignment to `self._record_num` in `agent.py` and found none besides `__init__`; it was never reassigned anywhere, ever, and `run_task.py` had no outer loop either. A real run could only ever attempt record 1, regardless of accuracy or how many records the intake held — not a tuning gap, an unbuilt feature. **Implemented 2026-08-12**, direct request ("make it attempt 1-5"): `_try_advance_tab()`'s existing last-tab-exhausted+Submit branch (which already clicked Submit from an earlier fix, but never did anything with `self._record_num` after) now calls two new pure functions — `_total_records_in()` (how many record blocks `_parse_records()` found; defaults to 1, so any non-multi-record source is unaffected) and `_next_record_or_finished()` (advance or genuinely-done) — and either increments `self._record_num` + re-refreshes the record cache (`self._record_just_advanced = True`) or ends the run cleanly (`self._task_finished = True`). Both flags are checked once at the top of `run()`'s main loop rather than at each of `_try_advance_tab`'s ~10 call sites, so none of their existing logic changed. Also added `--start_record`/`--end_record` to `run_task.py` (direct follow-up request) so a specific range (e.g. records 2-3 of a 5-record file) can be attempted without touching the intake file. **Real bug caught by the new tests, fixed in passing**: a stray local `import time` inside `_try_advance_tab` (pre-existing, harmless until this change added a second `time.sleep()` call in a different branch of the same function) shadowed the module-level import and raised `UnboundLocalError` — removed. 23 new regression tests (`tests/test_multi_record_advance.py`), full suite otherwise unaffected. **Not yet verified live** — per this project's standing rule, only the user runs the agent against the real form; this is unit-tested against a real `LLMAgent(dry_run=True)` instance with a mocked executor/observer, not confirmed against `car_insurance_form_wx.py` itself yet.

  **First real live run, same night, confirmed it works**: `Record 1/10 submitted — advancing to record 2` fired correctly, and the cache genuinely refreshed to record 2's own different data (Policy Number `PAI-2026-00442`, Policyholder Brenda Tompkins vs. record 1's `PAI-2026-00441`/James Delgado). Note the intake file actually has **10** records, not 5 — the mechanism doesn't care, it just uses whatever `_total_records_in()` finds. **New gap surfaced by this same run, not yet fixed**: `bc_fidelity.py`'s end-of-run report still hardcodes its comparison against record 1's expected values — the log shows it scoring the on-screen state (genuinely record 2's data, since the agent had legitimately advanced) against record 1's answer key, producing a wall of false "mismatches" (`Last Name typed='Tompkins' expected='Delgado'`, etc.) that look like agent errors but are actually a stale scoring assumption. `bc_fidelity.py`/`eval_metrics.py` need to know which record was actually on screen when the run ended, not just always check record 1. Flagged, not fixed tonight.

Still well short of target, but this is the first "no data yet" resolution this metric has ever had — see the four-questions note above.
- [x] `scope1_expected_vs_actual` — **Built 2026-08-11.** New `scripts/expected_vs_actual.py` — a complete, per-tab, per-field expected-vs-actual report for one record: every gold field shown as `match`/`mismatch`/`missing`, not just the up-to-10 mismatches `bc_fidelity.py`'s aggregate scoring already captured. Deliberately reuses `bc_fidelity`'s own intake parsing (`_parse_intake_record`), tab mapping (`_tab_of`), value normalization (`_normalize`), and metadata-field list (`_SKIP_FIELDS`) rather than duplicating comparison rules — metadata fields (`policy_number`, `policy_status`) are shown in the report for a complete audit trail but excluded from scored summary counts, exactly mirroring `bc_fidelity`'s own scoring semantics.

  CLI: `python scripts/expected_vs_actual.py --record 1` (defaults to the latest submission) or `--submission <path>`, plus `--json` for machine-readable output. `build_report()` is a pure function of its inputs (submission dict + intake path), directly unit-testable without any file I/O beyond reading the intake text.

  Verified against a real submission from earlier today's live run (42/173 fields filled): correctly reproduced the tab-by-tab breakdown — Policy 73%, Policyholder 97%, Vehicle 12%, Coverage 0% — matching that run's own logged summary, but now with exact per-field detail (which fields, what was expected, what was actually there) instead of just an aggregate number.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_expected_vs_actual.py` (14 tests) — match/mismatch/missing classification, value normalization, correct tab grouping, metadata-field exclusion from scored totals (caught two of my own test bugs before shipping: the synthetic test forgot `policy_status` is also metadata alongside `policy_number`, and used bare `"make"`/`"year"` instead of the real derived keys `"v_make"`/`"v_year"` — verified the actual parser output directly rather than guess at the fix), 0/0-division guard on tabs with no scored fields, and record-number selection.

Re-record with `scripts/recording_quality_gate.py` checked every few sessions, not just at the end. This is the single blocker for several downstream items — see `requires` below.
  - Progress: 2,966 steps / 5 sessions in `data/demos/eight_Tabs/` as of 2026-08-06 (~6% of the original, since-revised 50k target).
  - **Target formally lowered to 10,000 total traces, 2026-08-11, direct decision.** The original 50k figure was already flagged as provisional 2026-08-07/08 (sized without a real scaling curve, never actually committed to — the real plan agreed then was a moderate next batch of ~6,000-9,000 more samples). 10,000 is a concrete restatement of that same already-agreed range, not a new direction. Current real state as of this update: **2,974 clean steps** (4 verified-real sessions — see `learning_eight_tabs_data_audit_and_83pct_click_acc` and the 2026-08-11 session-audit note above) against the new 10,000 target — **~29.7% of the way there**, meaning roughly **7,000 more clean steps** need to be recorded. Every session must still pass the same real-typing / on-target / non-empty audit that caught 25 unusable sessions across the two audits so far — hitting 10,000 raw files is not the same as hitting 10,000 *usable* ones.
  - **Required step, not yet automated**: raw captures under `data/demos/*` have `action: null` — `mouse`/`keyboard` events aren't converted into a structured `action.action_type` until `scripts/backfill_actions.py --trace-dir <dir>` is run. Every analysis script (`recording_quality_gate.py`, `validate_transitions.py`, `bc_fidelity.py`) silently reads `action: {}` defaults on unbackfilled data and reports misleadingly — one full quality-gate pass on this batch was garbage (0% submit, 0% field coverage, no scroll) purely because backfill hadn't run yet, not because the recordings were bad. Run backfill before every quality-gate check on freshly recorded data.
  - **Code bug fixed**: `bc_fidelity._tab_of()` mapped 9 Policy-tab keys (`effective_date`, `expiration_date`, `agent_id`, `agent_name`, `agency_name`, `underwriter`, `renewal_flag`, `paperless`, `esign` — the ones without a `policy_` prefix) to "Other" instead of "Policy", silently under-crediting the 10%-weighted `tab_coverage` term of the fidelity score used in Thesis Ch.4. Fixed via an explicit `_UNPREFIXED_POLICY_KEYS` override.
  - **Scroll check reframed** (per correction: "the system scrolls, not the recorder"): `recording_quality_gate.py` no longer flags "NO SCROLL" — it now measures per-tab field coverage (did Tab-navigation actually reach every known field in a section) instead of counting scroll-wheel events. After the backfill fix above, real scroll actions were confirmed present in the raw captures anyway.
  - **Third bug found — user directly disputed the "0/5 reached Submit" reading ("I swear to God, I pressed Submit"), and was right**: `recording_quality_gate._clicked_element()` returned the *first* element in `state["elements"]` whose bbox contained the click point, not the most specific one. Elements overlap (window > panel > button) and the window is listed first in UIA capture order, so every click anywhere in the form silently resolved to the window element instead of the button actually clicked — `submit_reached` could never fire. Fixed to pick the smallest-area containing bbox. Real result: **3/5 sessions reached Submit**, not 0/5. Also incidentally surfaced that the recorder's live form window still has the old two-button "Submit & New" / "Submit" layout (bboxes ~1224-1335 / ~1348-1442) — the running process predates `scope1_unify_submit_buttons` and needs restarting to pick up the single-button fix.
  - **After all three fixes, this batch's real numbers**: field coverage 83-100% on Policy/Policyholder/Vehicle (the only 3 tabs `bc_fidelity._LABEL_TO_KEY` currently covers), tab order 100% consistent, 3/5 sessions reached Submit (the 2 that didn't were short 115-210 step sessions, plausibly practice/partial passes), transition-mapping accuracy 75.3% (below the 90% target — a new signal, invisible before the backfill fix), encoding ambiguity still 9.83% (target <5%).
  - **2026-08-06, early train-on-partial-batch experiment**: at the user's request, trained on this batch as-is (2,505 train / 442 val samples, `--epochs 50 --device cuda`) before the 50k campaign finished, to get a real read on data quality rather than wait blind. Result: best checkpoint at epoch 41, val_acc=0.824, val_click_acc=0.298 — plateaued in a 0.24-0.40 band from ~epoch 30 on, no clear upward trend by epoch 50. Roughly flat vs. the pre-existing committed checkpoint (epoch 65 on the old 19-session set: val_acc=0.684, val_click_acc=0.309) — not a regression, but not progress either, and well short of the 90% target. Consistent with the batch's own known issues (2/5 sessions partial, 75.3% transition-mapping accuracy). `model.pt` is git-tracked and currently shows as locally modified, not committed — the prior checkpoint is fully recoverable via `git checkout -- tasks/form_filling/model.pt` if needed. Reinforces: keep recording toward 50k rather than treat an early partial-batch model as representative.
  - **Tooling blind spot above: FIXED 2026-08-11** (`execution_bc_fidelity_tab_coverage_blind_spot`, full detail there). `_LABEL_TO_KEY` now covers all 8 tabs; `tabs_covered` is no longer structurally capped at 3.
  - **Session audit, 2026-08-11**: ran `scripts/backfill_actions.py --trace-dir data/demos/eight_Tabs` for real (previously only dry-run) — 3,291/3,291 steps backfilled cleanly, 0 errors. Then audited the 12 sessions recorded since the 2026-08-09 audit (which had archived 13/17 junk sessions) for the same problem. All 12 turned out unusable, for two different reasons: 9 had zero keyboard activity at all (identical to the prior junk pattern); the other 3 DID have real keystrokes but a direct check of their captured UI element labels showed why they'd looked promising and weren't — one (`session_20260810_033542`) captured a Firefox/YouTube browsing session, not the insurance form at all (the exact category of accidental off-target capture this project has hit before, this time in training data rather than a screenshot); the other two (`_035839`, `_092025`) have zero captured UI elements whatsoever — empty, broken captures. All 12 moved to `data/demos/eight_Tabs_pre_typing_archive/` alongside the original 13, leaving exactly the 4 previously-verified-clean sessions in `eight_Tabs/`. **Net: no usable recording progress since 2026-08-09** — the 50k-step count has not actually advanced; every session recorded in between was unusable for one of three distinct reasons now documented. This is the real, current state to plan the next recording session around.
  - **First real quality-gate report since the tab-coverage fix, 2026-08-11** (previously impossible — see `execution_bc_fidelity_tab_coverage_blind_spot` for why the tool's own numbers were vacuous defaults before this): on the 4 clean sessions, avg tabs covered **4.0/8** (not 3/8 — real signal now, still well short of full coverage), Submit rate **57%** (4/7 counting an older combined run), transition-mapping accuracy **42.7%** (target ≥90%), encoding ambiguity **14.01%** (target <5%). Tool's own verdict: finish each pass through Submit rather than stopping partway, some recorded actions have no visible effect (possible junk clicks), and too many fields share the same label+type (label collisions like the ones just fixed in `_LABEL_TO_KEY` are a plausible contributor). This is genuinely worse-looking than the previous fake "100%" figures — but it's real, and real numbers are what data-quality decisions have to be made from.

Needs one live run allowed to actually finish.

  **First real baseline, 2026-08-13**, direct framing from the user ("we can't necessarily call this a productive tool if it is slower than the person we're trying to automate this for"): `run_task_20260813_005301.log` measured **2.02s/step, ~30 steps/min**. Root-caused the actual bottleneck rather than assuming it's the fixed inter-step delay: `LLM Dependency` was **75%** (target <5%) — three of every four steps needed a full LLM round-trip, and that dominates real wall-clock cost. Traced to `agent.py`'s `_HIGH_CONF = 1.01  # effectively: never skip the LLM` (~L2383) — a deliberate gate from when the legacy action-type head reliably collapsed to guessing "click." Checked whether the new semantic checkpoint changes this calculus before proposing anything: it doesn't yet — its own type accuracy is only 51.8% (see `learning_action_accuracy_90`), nowhere near reliable enough to trust deciding WHAT alone. Flipping the gate now would trade real correctness for speed. See new task `execution_llm_call_cost_reduction` above for the real, not-yet-tried levers.

  **CORRECTION, same night, before anything above was acted on**: "LLM Dependency 75%" does NOT mean 75% of steps paid a real network round-trip — checked directly against the same log: `grep -c "LLM call skipped"` → **124**, `grep -c "LLM prompt size"` (only logged immediately before a real API call fires) → **0**. Zero real LLM calls happened in that entire 116-step run. `LLM Dependency` measures which CODE PATH decided the value (the LLM-merge path vs. the transformer alone), not whether a network call actually happened — `agent.py`'s direct-lookup fast path was skipping the real LLM call essentially 100% of the time. The `execution_llm_call_cost_reduction` lever above was based on a real metric name meaning something other than what it sounds like — not a real bottleneck in this run.

  **Real bottleneck, found the same night via a per-step timing breakdown of `run_task_20260813_224241.log`**: median step time 3s, mean 2.84s. `STEP_DELAY = 1.0s` (fixed, unconditional, every single step) alone accounts for ~114 of ~326 total seconds across 114 steps — roughly HALF the run's total wall-clock time, confirming `run_task.py`'s own comment. The rest splits between real UIA observation/execution overhead and a smaller (~9% of total time) but real cost concentrated in 2-3 specific structural failures per run (a combobox whose dropdown never renders, costing ~9s per attempt; fields where a typed value doesn't verify/stick, costing ~1s per retry and still ending up unfilled either way) — not spread evenly, and arguably more a correctness bug than a speed one.

  **Fixed 2026-08-14, direct request ("fix Speed without sacrificing reliability")**: replaced the blind `time.sleep(self.step_delay)` at the main loop's single highest-frequency call site (the success-path end-of-step delay) with `_adaptive_settle_wait()` — polls the observed state at short intervals and returns as soon as two consecutive reads agree the UI has stopped changing, instead of always waiting the full budget. Bounded by real wall-clock time (`time.time()`, not an assumed per-poll cost), so it can overshoot the old budget by at most one poll interval, never wait meaningfully longer. Deliberately scoped to ONLY this one call site — the ~25 other, smaller fractional `step_delay` sleeps scattered through specific retry/redirect branches were left untouched, to avoid destabilizing logic not individually verified safe to change in the same pass; the failure-path sleep right next to it (a genuinely failed execution) also deliberately kept its full blind wait. 11 new tests (`tests/test_adaptive_settle_wait.py`), using a fake deterministic clock so timing behavior is tested precisely without real delays. Full suite: 772+ passed, 0 failed (two known resource-contention flakes — `test_ghost_overlay`, `test_emergency_stop_hotkey` — reproduced clean in isolation both times, confirmed unrelated). Not yet verified live.

  **A speed feature built the same night on top of `FormFillerPlugin` was found, later, to be built on the wrong foundation** — `run_task.py` (the script actually used for every live run) never sets a `task_plugin` at all. Worse, `disable_auto_handlers=True` (which `run_task.py` does set) is itself deliberate: *"we want to see the pure transformer with no rescue (honest navigation test)"* — the project intentionally excludes exactly the kind of deterministic heuristic that plugin represents, so the model's own learned capability is what actually gets measured. That plugin-based work stays in the tree (see `execution_plan_then_replay`) as real, tested, harmless, opt-in code — it just doesn't apply to the path actually tested against.

  **Corrected 2026-08-14, same night: extended the real `_adaptive_settle_wait` pattern into "OPT2"** — the transformer-navigation + LLM-value-oracle mechanism that's actually active whenever `disable_auto_handlers=True` (`agent.py`, gated around L2398). Confirmed the underlying diagnosis above still holds on the real path: `_ask_llm()`'s own internal lookup-cache fast path (L7599-7625) is completely ungated by `disable_auto_handlers` and fires unconditionally, so the "124 LLM calls skipped, 0 real network calls" finding transfers — the mistake was the foundation, not the diagnosis. Two other directions were evaluated and explicitly rejected: batching the transformer's top-N WHERE-candidates from one forward pass (structurally cheap, but the existing deterministic no-op-click detection + `NavigationProtocol`-driven redirect escalation, `agent.py` L4866-4892/2688-2690/2819+, already covers this case, and there's no existing measurement of whether the model's own rank-2 choice is any good); batching the LLM's WHAT-decisions across multiple fields in one call (`_SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `_parse_llm_response` are both architecturally single-field; would need a real prompt-contract redesign and a new stable per-field identifier, for a payoff limited to the minority of fields that reach a real network call).

  A real correction found in the process: `_plugin_active = (self._task_plugin is not None) or self._pure_transformer or self._no_autohandlers` (L1570) — since `run_task.py` sets `disable_auto_handlers=True`, `_plugin_active` is *also* always `True` on the real live path, meaning every sleep site gated `if not _plugin_active:`/`if not self._no_autohandlers:` is dead code for `run_task.py`, never reached at all. Roughly half of the raw ~49 remaining `time.sleep(self.step_delay * N)` sites fall into this category — the real reachable count was smaller than the "~25 other sites" framing above implied.

  Read all remaining candidate sites directly (not assumed safe) before converting. Excluded, with reasons: every site next to a real tab-strip switch (same "checking the screen too soon after a tab switch" incident already named above — `agent.py` L2377, 4257, 4324, 4341); the record-reset wait (`# let the form's own reset finish`, L7361); the LLM-unavailable retry backoff (a different purpose — retry pacing, not UI-settle — L2539); the verify-at-fill retry loop (L4845, 4863 — its own long history of subtle, only-live-visible bugs, held out as a separate, later, individually-isolated follow-up); combobox open/close handling (L2648, 3564, 4536, plus the separate combobox-open destructive-button-guard site at L3367 — already through ten documented bugfix rounds elsewhere in this project, handled separately with its own coverage); one site with a shared sleep across two different branches (scroll vs. real tab-advance, L3784, needs splitting before either half is judgeable); one site with no confirmed follow-up verification found yet (L4310).

  Converted 12 confirmed-safe sites — every one a plain Tab keystroke or same-page click immediately followed by `continue`, no tab-strip switch, no dropdown state: the dialog-guard Escape, two leave-blank Tabs, the pointer-drift-reclick Tab, the combobox-known-blank Tab, the hallucinated-target-outside-form guard, the destructive-button-guard navigate-Tab, the backward-tab-click guard, and the repeat-action guard — plus three redirect sites (reclick-streak, combobox-reclick, low-confidence-fallback) that were **already manually implementing** "sleep blind, then `self._observe()` and check the click actually landed" by hand — the exact shape `_adaptive_settle_wait` automates, making these the same category of obviously-correct change as the original site, not a new judgment call. 18 new tests (`tests/test_opt2_settle_wait_batch1.py`) — direct source-level verification per site (matching this project's own established pattern for logic embedded in the un-extracted `run()` method, e.g. `tests/test_redirect_click_destructive_button_guard.py`) that each converted site now calls `_adaptive_settle_wait` and each excluded site still doesn't, plus a `_FakeClock` mirror-function test for the three "wait then verify landed" sites proving that shape still settles early/bounds correctly. Full suite: 873 passed, 9 skipped, 0 failed.

  **Honestly unverified**: the magnitude of this win, unlike the original site (measured at ~50% of wall-clock time), is unproven and likely modest per site — no per-branch frequency data exists yet for these lower-frequency sites, and no real, substantial `run_task.py` log was available on disk this session to pull one from (only tiny 0-step recovery-period logs). A "safe-looking" site could still be secretly load-bearing for reasons invisible from static reading (real Windows UIA/wx redraw latency only visible live) — the same category this project has already been burned by once. Convert-a-batch-then-verify-live, not batch-then-batch, is the intended cadence; the remaining ~half of sites not yet traced back to their nearest enclosing gate are real future work, not assumed already covered. Not yet verified live.

perception.

Concretely: confirm `ExcelObserver` (already proven, `scope2_excel_perception_proven`) and a new Excel `DataSource`/executor slot cleanly into `LLMAgent`'s existing constructor seams the same way `NotepadDataSource`/UIA observer do today, with zero Scope #1-specific code required to change. *requires: `scope1_record_advance_5`, `scope1_e2e_metric`* (per this section's own header — Scope #2 work is deliberately sequenced after Scope #1 lands, and this architecture-integration pass is the first real Scope #2 task once that gate opens).
- [ ] `scope2_coworker_codebase_integration` — **Started 2026-08-14, direct request** ("This is my coworker's progress for Scope #2, I need to integrate the codebase" — `github.com/RJGanzon/Intern`). A real, separately-developed implementation of cross-app spreadsheet↔form transfer, 181 tests, 8 mock-portal variants for robustness testing, real ablation studies. Read fully (README + core implementation files) before touching anything, not skimmed.

  **Real discrepancy found and flagged, not silently resolved**: this section's own header says "Web Form → Excel"; the coworker's system reads FROM Excel and writes INTO a web form — the reverse. Left as an open item to reconcile, not assumed either way.

  **Architecturally very different from Scope #1, not a drop-in.** Ours: a transformer learns WHERE to click from raw UI sequences, general-purpose across a multi-step, multi-tab task. Theirs: schema-matching — a tiny 1,121-parameter network scores every (spreadsheet column, form field) pair on 17 hand-engineered features (semantic similarity via the same `all-MiniLM-L6-v2` we already use, lexical distance, value-shape, structural, positional, option-overlap), then the Hungarian algorithm finds the optimal one-to-one assignment, abstaining when two candidates can't be told apart. Only one component (the matcher) is learned; the rest (recorder, reconciler, rule induction, resolver, executor) is deterministic — the opposite philosophy from "let the Transformer and Agent work throughout." Their own stated Limitations section is explicit their system is scoped to single-page forms, no multi-step navigation at all — it could not drive our actual 8-tab insurance form on its own.

  **Decision, given directly to the user as a genuine fork**: how to integrate — full merge, adopt as Scope #2's canonical implementation, extract specific pieces, or bring the whole thing in and figure out fit later. User chose **"getting both codebase's good sides"** — a real synthesis, not a full merge or a full pass-over. Then, offered a concrete first step (bring in the stack-agnostic matching core and test it against our own data, vs. the browser-stack side, vs. holding everything for a live Scope #1 run first) — user chose **"start there."**

All four use a relative-depth `parents[1]`-style bootstrap that resolves correctly automatically as long as their own internal folder nesting is preserved — copied byte-for-byte into `components/scope2/{features,model,resolver,labeling}/`, zero import-line edits, zero risk of a transcription bug.

Wires Scope #2 into the Electron app's existing "Workflow section"/"Play" mechanism (`app_electron/`) as its own runnable entry, without a deeper `LLMAgent`-level system merge (still explicitly out of scope, unchanged from every prior pass).

  **Read the existing architecture directly before designing anything**: a "capsule" (`components/agent/capsule.py`'s `WorkflowCapsule`) was hardcoded to mean "a `.pt` checkpoint for the same `LLMAgent`" — `app/recorder_bridge.py`'s `run_capsule()` always spawned `python run_task.py --model <checkpoint>`, `isfile()`-gated on that checkpoint existing. Scope #2 has no such checkpoint (its matcher trains fresh, in-process, from `automate.py`'s own stage 4), so it couldn't fit that schema without either breaking the gate or misusing `model_path`.

  **Fix, generic rather than Scope #2-specific** (matches this project's own "don't hardcode for tasks" rule applied at the app layer): `WorkflowCapsule` gained four fields — `kind` ("agent", the original default, or "script"), `entrypoint`, `args`, `cwd` — plus one pure method, `launch_command(repo_root)`, that returns the `(argv, cwd)` to `Popen` for either kind. `run_capsule()` now resolves a capsule **by name** through an **injectable** `CapsuleRegistry` (`Bridge(registry=...)` — same dependency-injection pattern already used for `agent.py`'s `_resolve_control_via_point`'s `uia_mod`/`win32gui_mod`/`user32`), and calls `launch_command()` instead of hardcoding `run_task.py` — the existing, hard-won Popen flags (`stdin=DEVNULL`, `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, unbuffered `-u`, line-by-line stdout pumping into `logs/capsule_activity.log`) are all reused verbatim for both kinds, not duplicated. `tasks/registry.json` gained one new entry, `"Sheet-to-Portal Matcher"` (`kind: "script"`, `entrypoint: components/scope2/automate.py`, `args: --variant v0_base --commit --show --limit 5`) — `--limit 5` mirrors Scope #1's own "one clean record is enough to showcase" bar; `--commit --show` gives real parity with Scope #1's Play (an actual fill+verify+save against the mock portal in a visible browser, not a silent dry run — safe here since the mock portal write is local and disposable, unlike Scope #1's real target app). `route()` (used to pick a checkpoint for `LLMAgent`) is structurally untouched: the new entry's empty `trigger_keywords`/`trigger_apps` mean it can never be returned by `route()`, proven directly in `tests/test_capsule_launch_command.py::TestRouteNeverReturnsAScriptCapsule`, not just assumed from the code shape.

  **Electron side updated to match**: `app_electron/main.js`/`preload.js`'s capsule IPC (`capsule-run`, `capsule-set-current`, `capsule-run-current`) now carries a capsule *name* through the whole chain instead of a bare `model_path` string. `renderer.js`'s Play panel (`loadCapsuleIntoSlot`) hides the Checkpoint dropdown/Deploy button entirely for a `kind="script"` capsule (there's no swappable checkpoint) and instead shows exactly what will run (`entrypoint` + `args`), so clicking Play is never a surprise. The Workflow section (previously only ever listing `data/demos/<group>/` folders scanned off disk) now also renders one card per `kind="script"` registry capsule, unconditionally — even when zero demo groups exist — since Scope #2 has no `data/demos/` group to hang off of at all.

  **`tests/test_recorder_bridge_capsule_run.py` genuinely rewritten**, not just extended — every test's call shape changed from `bridge.run_capsule(<bare checkpoint path>)` to `bridge.run_capsule(<capsule name>)` against an injected, seeded registry, plus a new `TestRunScriptCapsule` class covering the `kind="script"` Popen shape and its own missing-entrypoint error path. New `tests/test_capsule_launch_command.py` (11 tests) covers `launch_command()` directly for both kinds, including path-normalization edge cases caught live on Windows (`os.path.join` doesn't normalize interior `/` in a relative `model_path`, fixed by resolving both sides with `Path(...).resolve()` before comparing in the test, not a code bug). `tests/test_capsule_emoji_field.py` extended with the same backward-compatibility discipline its existing emoji tests use — a registry entry written before these four fields existed still loads cleanly, defaulting `kind` to `"agent"`.

Full project test suite re-run end to end after all of the above for regressions.
