Disproven directly: `IsWindowVisible()`/`GetWindowRect()` both correctly reflected the change at the real Win32 level immediately (confirmed even after a full second's wait), but `ControlFromPoint` at the same point kept resolving to the window's *stale* prior state regardless — UI Automation's view of this particular window (Tkinter, `overrideredirect`, layered) did not track its own live visibility or position once already established.
  2. The overlay's existing `hide_cursor()`/`hide_caret()` (canvas-only clears) — no effect for the same reason `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` already didn't help UIA specifically: the window's mere *existence*, not its drawn content or visibility flag, is what was being hit.

  **The only mechanism confirmed, live, to actually work**: genuinely stopping the overlay (destroying its window) and starting a fresh one afterward. Verified directly: `ControlFromPoint` at a point the overlay was covering found the real control again immediately once `stop()` had returned. New `GhostOverlay.hide_for_uia_read()`/`restore_after_uia_read()` do exactly this. Real, measurable overhead (thread teardown + a fresh `Tk()`, confirmed live at ~55ms + ~85ms — cheaper than feared, but non-zero), so scoped carefully:
  - `agent.py`'s `_select_combobox_value_via_keyboard()` now calls this **once** for the whole function call (before the type-ahead/Down/Up search begins, restored in a `finally` after it ends) — not once per keystroke read, which would have multiplied the cost by up to ~40x.
  - `executor.py`'s `_try_uia_invoke()` deliberately does **not** get this fix — it runs on every single click throughout an entire run, where paying this cost per click would be a real, unacceptable slowdown. Left as a documented, known gap: this optimization silently no-ops (falls through to the working pyautogui path) whenever the overlay is active. A cheaper fix for this specific call site (e.g. resizing the overlay to a small, positioned window instead of full-screen, so most on-screen points are never covered at all) is a real architectural option but wasn't attempted tonight — correctness for the combobox fallback (previously guaranteed to fail) mattered more under time pressure than a speed optimization that already has a working fallback.

  Regression tests: `tests/test_ghost_overlay.py` gained `TestHideForUiaRead` (3 tests, mocked stop/start delegation) and extended the file's one real-Tk-window subprocess test to exercise a genuine `hide_for_uia_read()`/`restore_after_uia_read()` cycle against a real window (confirms actual thread teardown/recreation, not just mocks). `tests/test_combobox_keyboard_fallback.py` gained `TestGhostOverlayStoppedForTheWholeCall` (4 tests: hide/restore called exactly once regardless of keystroke count; restore skipped when hide reports nothing was running; restore still happens after an exception; no crash when `.ghost` doesn't exist at all). Full suite: **588 passed** / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures, no crash.

  Not yet re-verified through a complete real Play-launched run (verified directly against the raw mechanism — overlay start/stop/UIA resolution — and via the real `_select_combobox_value_via_keyboard()` code path with a mocked ghost, but not yet via an actual live capsule run reaching this exact field) — that's the next real confirmation, alongside seeing whether `_combobox_dropdown_fail_counts`'s existing 2-strike back-off (never actually disproven, just never fully observed) behaves as expected on a field that's now genuinely fixable.

  **REVERTED the same day, after a severe live regression — reported plainly: "It got worse, still was not solved and also why couldn't I click a damn thing in the form?"** The fix above was verified exactly once per process before shipping (one hide/restore cycle, in a subprocess-isolated test). A real run needed it TWICE in quick succession — two failed `'Policy Status'` attempts before `_combobox_dropdown_fail_counts`'s 2-strike back-off correctly gave up and moved on (confirmed working as designed, directly in the log: `"[OPT2] 'Policy Status' failed to fill 2 times in a row ... marking attempted and moving on."`). Immediately after moving on to the next field (`'Policy Type'`) and clicking to open its dropdown, `logs/capsule_activity.log` recorded: `"Tcl_AsyncDelete: async handler deleted by the wrong thread"` — the exact Tcl/Tk corruption signature `tests/test_ghost_overlay.py`'s own module docstring already documents as a precursor to a hard, process-wide crash (`"Windows fatal exception: code 0x80000003"`) when a Tk root's teardown isn't strictly confined to a single, isolated process. The run then produced zero further output for 17 seconds until the user force-killed it via the emergency-stop hotkey — direct, live evidence of a corrupted, no-longer-click-through, full-screen topmost window sitting over the whole desktop, blocking every click: *"We're not even filling the task anymore. After properly inputting Policy Number, it can't fucking click anymore."*

  This is a worse bug than the one being fixed. Repeated in-process `stop()`→`start()` cycling of this Tk thread is not safe, even though a single cycle, tested in isolation, genuinely is. Reverted immediately: `_select_combobox_value_via_keyboard()`'s `_live_value()` is back to a plain, unguarded read (the ghost-overlay interference this whole entry diagnoses is real and still unfixed at the read level); `GhostOverlay.hide_for_uia_read()`/`restore_after_uia_read()` were removed from `ghost_overlay.py` entirely rather than left defined-but-unused — a working-in-isolation, dangerous-in-practice method sitting in the codebase is a trap for a future "just call it once more" change to re-trigger the exact same crash class. The diagnosis in this entry (the overlay's full-screen window intercepting `ControlFromPoint` for its entire lifetime) remains correct and valuable; only the fix attempt is reverted.

  What actually bounds the damage now, same as before this whole investigation started: `_combobox_dropdown_fail_counts`'s 2-strike back-off, confirmed working live in the very same run that surfaced this crash — a combobox whose dropdown never renders costs at most two ~17s attempts before the agent marks it attempted and moves on, not an infinite loop. A wrong-but-bounded fallback beats a fix that can silently take over the user's entire screen input. A real fix for the underlying UIA-interference bug (e.g. resizing the overlay to a small, positioned window instead of a full-screen one, so most on-screen points are never covered and nothing ever needs to be destroyed/recreated) is a genuine, separate piece of architectural work, not a same-day patch — noted here for whenever that's picked up.

  Regression tests: the `TestHideForUiaRead` class in `tests/test_ghost_overlay.py`, the extended real-Tk hide/restore assertions in that file's one subprocess lifecycle script, and the `TestGhostOverlayStoppedForTheWholeCall` class in `tests/test_combobox_keyboard_fallback.py` were all removed along with the code they tested — they were testing a mechanism that no longer exists, not a regression guard for anything still shipped. Full suite back to **581 passed** / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures, no crash — matching the count from immediately before this fix was ever added.

  **Very next live run, reported plainly: "The Agent couldn't click on the form at all. It's frozen shut."** Directly checked the wxForm process (`Get-Process ... Responding`) — `True`, not actually hung at the OS level, and `capsule_activity.log` showed no `Tcl_AsyncDelete` this time (the crash mechanism above is genuinely gone). What the log showed instead: `'Policy Number'` filled correctly, then `'Policy Status'` hit the (still-unfixed, expected) dead end — 3.2s click-and-poll, then **~11 straight seconds of nothing but Down/Down/Down.../Up/Up/Up keyboard-only actions** (`_select_combobox_value_via_keyboard()` always burning its full budget, since the ghost overlay still breaks every value read, exactly as this entry already documents) with zero clicks and zero visible on-screen change, since the dropdown never renders. From outside, watching the screen, that's indistinguishable from "frozen" even though the process never actually stopped responding — a real, valid UX report even though it wasn't the same crash class as before.

  Since this fallback currently has a **provable 0% success rate** whenever a ghost overlay is present (and `agent.py` hardcodes `ghost_cursor=True` for every real, non-dry-run construction — so every real live run), and re-attempting the stop/restart fix again the same day was clearly not the move, the safe fix was to stop calling it under that exact condition rather than try to repair the read again: `_select_combobox_value_via_keyboard()` now returns `False` immediately, with zero keyboard actions, whenever `self._executor.ghost is not None`. Falls straight through to the existing, already-proven-working `_combobox_dropdown_fail_counts` 2-strike escalation — same eventual outcome (field marked attempted, agent moves on), but the "looks frozen" window drops from ~11s down to the original ~3.2s poll per attempt. Scoped precisely on `ghost is not None`, not a blanket disable — dry_run and any future `ghost_cursor=False` construction still get the real fallback logic, unaffected; only the one condition that's actually proven broken is skipped. No threading, no window lifecycle, no Tcl/Tk touched at all this time — the lowest-risk change available given what's already gone wrong twice today.

  Existing tests all constructed their mock executor via plain `MagicMock()`, which auto-creates `.ghost` as a truthy sub-mock — meaning the new guard would have silently short-circuited every existing test before it ever reached the logic it was written to exercise, without any of them failing (a false-confidence trap, not a crash). Fixed by having `_make_executor_mock()` explicitly set `ghost = None`, and the two `MagicMock()`-direct tests in `TestGracefulDegradation` likewise, so all of them continue to test the real search algorithm exactly as before. New `TestSkippedWhileGhostOverlayIsActive` (2 tests): confirms zero keyboard actions when `ghost` is present, and confirms the fallback still runs normally when it's `None`. Full suite: **583 passed** / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures, no crash.

  Still open, deliberately not fixed today: the actual UIA-read bug this whole entry diagnoses. The keyboard fallback is fully correct, verified-working logic (confirmed live, the very same day) that simply cannot currently prove its own success while a ghost overlay covers the screen — it's disabled under exactly that condition, not deleted. A real fix (e.g. a small, positioned overlay window instead of full-screen, so `ControlFromPoint` mostly never needs to see through it at all) remains genuine future architectural work.

- [ ] `execution_reassert_backoff_unidentified_foreign_window` — **Found live 2026-08-10, directly following the fix above.** With the keyboard-fallback skip in place (11s cut to ~3.2s per dead combobox), the very next live run still reported: *"Still the same problem the fucking screen is still frozen, you can't click on the form after Policy Number."* Read the log directly rather than assuming a repeat of the earlier crash: no `Tcl_AsyncDelete` this time (that mechanism stayed fixed), and the wxForm process itself was `Responding: True` when checked directly — not actually hung at the OS level.

  What the log actually showed: `'Policy Type'` failed to open its dropdown twice, then `'Policy Status'` failed twice more — four unrelated combobox failures back to back, ~19 seconds total, each individually fast (~5s) but stacking into one long stretch with nothing visibly filling in. That's a separate, already-known class of flakiness (some comboboxes just don't reliably render on click — the same open question `execution_combobox_never_opens_keyboard_fallback` and its history already track), not a new regression from today's fix.

  The concerning new detail: `_reassert_form_window()`'s streak-breaker logged `"NOT re-stealing foreground -- hwnd=197334 has held focus across 2/3 consecutive steps..."` twice. Asked the user directly what actually happened when they tried to click, rather than assume — direct answer: *"Clicked the form, literally nothing happened."* That single fact rules out the streak-breaker's own stated assumption ("e.g. the user reaching for Stop") — the user clicked the visible form itself, not some other window, and nothing happened at all, meaning some window OTHER than the form was silently sitting on top of it and swallowing that click, well before the user ever tried.

  Tried to identify hwnd 197334 after the fact (`Get-Process | Where MainWindowHandle -eq 197334`) — no match, but this is not conclusive: `Process.MainWindowHandle` only resolves *decorated* top-level windows, and the ghost overlay is deliberately `overrideredirect` (borderless, no taskbar presence) — exactly the shape of window this lookup method cannot find whether or not it's actually the culprit. Real suspects, in order of plausibility: (1) the ghost overlay itself, somehow holding `GetForegroundWindow()` status despite `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` (which governs mouse hit-testing, a separate Windows concept from "foreground window" — no contradiction if both are simultaneously true); (2) a same-process wx dialog (e.g. a stray modal) that the existing modal-dismiss logic a few lines below the streak-breaker was never reached to handle, since the streak-breaker's `return` at streak≥2 happens **before** that same-PID check ever runs — a real, structural gap, independent of whether this specific incident was that; (3) some unrelated third-party window (a notification, a sync-client popup) coincidentally grabbing focus. No live evidence yet distinguishes these — the bare hwnd number the warning used to log is useless once the process has exited, which is exactly what happened here.

  Rather than guess a fix for an unidentified window a second time in one day, added **diagnostic-only** logging: the backoff warning now looks up and includes the foreign window's title, class name, and whether it's owned by the *same process* as the locked form (reusing the `win32process` pattern already used a few lines below for the modal-dialog check) — best-effort, wrapped so a lookup failure (window already gone, permissions) can never prevent the actual safety-critical behavior (backing off) from happening. Zero behavior change otherwise. The next occurrence will say definitively whether this is the ghost overlay, a same-process dialog, or something else entirely — and if it's a same-process dialog, that also confirms the structural gap above needs its own fix (moving the same-PID modal check ahead of the streak-breaker's early return, or running it regardless of streak).

  Regression tests: `tests/test_reassert_foreground_streak_breaker.py` gained `TestBackoffDiagnosticLogging` (3 tests — title/class appear in the log; a same-process window is clearly flagged `same_process_as_form=True` vs. `False` for an unrelated one; a lookup failure never prevents backing off). Full suite: **586 passed** / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures, no crash. Diagnosis only — genuinely not yet fixed, waiting on the next live occurrence to identify the actual window before deciding what the real fix is.

  **Identified and fixed the very next live run.** The new diagnostic logging did its job: `"NOT re-stealing foreground -- hwnd=1966806 title='tk' class='TkTopLevel' same_process_as_form=False"` — `title='tk'` and `class='TkTopLevel'` are the literal, unmistakable Tkinter defaults. This is the ghost overlay itself, confirmed, not a same-process dialog and not an unrelated third-party window.

  Verified live, directly, exactly what was happening: `GetForegroundWindow()` checked immediately before vs. after `GhostOverlay().start()` showed the overlay becomes the OS foreground/active window the instant it's created — a normal side effect of creating any new `-topmost`, visible top-level window, and completely independent of `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` (which only ever governed mouse *hit-testing* — whether a click lands on this window — never window *activation*, a separate Windows concept). `_make_click_through()` applies `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` only *after* the window already exists and has already been activated, so it never had any effect on this.

  This combined with the streak-breaker (an earlier fix, same day, for an unrelated real incident) to produce the reported bug: `_reassert_form_window()` correctly reclaims foreground the first time (its designed behavior for a single stray drift), but the overlay keeps taking it back — and once the *same* foreign window recurs, the streak-breaker deliberately stops fighting for it, assuming a human is deliberately using another app. Direct user report: *"Clicked the form, literally nothing happened."*

  **Fixed with a second, independent Win32 extended-window-style flag: `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`** — added to the exact same `SetWindowLongW` call that already sets `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT`/`WS_EX_LAYERED` in `_make_click_through()`. This flag tells Windows the window should never become foreground/active through normal means at all. Verified live, precisely targeting the actual failure mode rather than just the flag's existence: `GetForegroundWindow()` still shows the overlay winning the *very first* activation at creation time (expected — `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` is applied after that initial activation already happened, and `_reassert_form_window()` already handles a single one-time steal correctly by design, so this doesn't need fixing). The part that matters — simulated `SetForegroundWindow()` reclaim (standing in for `_reassert_form_window()`'s own reclaim call) followed by 5 full seconds of polling across simulated steps — showed foreground staying exactly where it was reclaimed to, with **zero** recurrence, versus the original bug's confirmed pattern of recurring across 2-3+ consecutive steps.

  Regression test: `tests/test_ghost_overlay.py::TestClickThroughWindowStyle::test_sets_ws_ex_noactivate` — confirms the flag is present in the style bits `SetWindowLongW` is actually called with (same mocked-`user32` pattern the sibling `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` test already uses). Full suite: **587 passed** / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures, no crash.

  Deliberately a small, single-flag, no-threading, no-window-lifecycle change — the opposite shape of the fix that caused today's earlier crash — and verified against the *actual reported failure mode* (recurring theft after reclaim) with real, live before/after measurements, not just confirming the style bit gets set. Not yet re-verified through a live Play run reaching this exact scenario (the next real click is the actual end-to-end confirmation) — but this is the first fix today with direct, mechanism-level live evidence behind it before being shipped, rather than after something broke.

  **The very next live run, reported plainly: "Still unable to click anything whatsoever check most recent logs."** Read the log directly. Good news first: the `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` fix above genuinely held — `"Re-asserted form foreground"` succeeded twice in this run, with **zero** `"NOT re-stealing foreground"` backoff warnings anywhere in the log. The recurring-theft bug is confirmed gone. But the user still couldn't click, and killed the run only ~13 seconds in, ~1 second after the last logged action.

  One concrete clue pointed the way: the user *could* trigger the emergency-stop hotkey (a global OS-level hook, independent of any window's focus) but apparently could not click. That specifically implicates mouse click-routing, not window activation — a different mechanism than what was just fixed. Rather than theorize again, tested it directly and empirically for the first time this session: spawned a real, disposable Notepad window as a safe click target, started the ghost overlay, showed the cursor icon at a specific point, then fired a **real `pyautogui.click()`** at that exact point and checked which window ended up with `GetForegroundWindow()`.

  Result: the click did **not** reach Notepad. `GetForegroundWindow()` after the click was the overlay's own `'tk'` window. A real, physical mouse click aimed at the drawn ghost-cursor icon was swallowed by the overlay instead of passing through — directly contradicting `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT`, and contradicting `ghost_overlay.py`'s own module docstring, which names click-through as *"the one thing this MUST get right."* That guarantee had only ever been checked by confirming the Windows style bits get set (`TestClickThroughWindowStyle`) — never against an actual click on actual drawn (non-color-keyed) content. Plausible mechanism: `WS_EX_LAYERED` (required for the `-transparentcolor` trick) has its own, separate hit-testing rules for opaque vs. color-keyed pixels that can take precedence over `WS_EX_TRANSPARENT` specifically where real, non-transparent-colored pixels are drawn — exactly where the cursor/caret shapes are. Since the ghost cursor is drawn exactly where the agent just acted — precisely where a user watching the run would naturally try to click too — this directly explains the repeated "can't click the form" reports, including this one, which happened *after* the correct, verified `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` fix for the unrelated foreground-theft bug.

  **This is the third distinct, confirmed, live-verified bug traced to this one feature in a single session**: (1) breaks every UIA read the whole time it runs; (2) steals and re-steals OS foreground; (3) swallows real physical clicks aimed at its own drawn content. None of the three have a quick, safe, well-understood fix remaining — the two attempted so far cost one severe crash and consumed most of a night's work. Rather than attempt a fourth patch to increasingly subtle Win32/DWM layered-window hit-testing semantics under continued time pressure, **disabled the feature entirely**: `LLMAgent.__init__` now constructs its `ActionExecutor` with `ghost_cursor=False` (was `True`, unconditionally, for every real live run). Reverts to `pyautogui`'s default behavior — moving the real OS cursor for clicks — the same proven, boring mechanism this project used before the ghost cursor existed.

  This one change also retroactively resolves the two "workaround" fixes shipped earlier today for symptoms of the same root cause, without touching either of them: with no overlay ever constructed in a real run, `self._executor.ghost` is `None`, so `_select_combobox_value_via_keyboard()`'s ghost-present skip (added a few entries above) simply never triggers and the keyboard fallback runs its real, already-verified-working logic again — and `executor.py`'s `_try_uia_invoke()` optimization, silently dead the entire time the overlay existed, starts actually working again too. Both were left in place rather than removed (see that entry's own reasoning) — dormant, correct, defensive code for whenever the overlay is properly redesigned and re-enabled.

  Regression test: `tests/test_agent_ghost_cursor_disabled.py` — patches `agent.executor.ActionExecutor` (where `agent.py`'s local `from agent.executor import ActionExecutor` actually resolves at call time, not a module-level attribute of `agent.agent`) and asserts `ghost_cursor=False` is what `LLMAgent.__init__` actually passes, so this can't silently flip back to `True` in a future refactor without that being a deliberate, visible decision. Full suite: **588 passed** / 9 skipped / same 2 pre-existing unrelated tesseract failures, no crash.

  Honest about what's lost: the orange ghost cursor/caret visual — a cosmetic nicety, and one the user had *already* separately reported never being able to find on screen even before any of this (never investigated, now moot). Not lost: anything about correctness or safety — `_try_uia_invoke()`'s real-cursor-skip optimization for supported controls still exists and still applies when a UIA action succeeds; only the purely visual indicator of where a *simulated* click landed is gone, and the real OS cursor visibly moving during automation (standard `pyautogui` behavior, unremarkable before this feature existed) takes its place. **Confirmed live the same night** — one more real run first surfaced a separate, unrelated issue (the Notepad window holding the source intake data wasn't open, so the record cache had 0 fields and every field was correctly left blank — not a code bug, just missing setup; reopened `data_entry_tasks/data_entry_intake.txt` in Notepad and the agent immediately began typing real values again). With that resolved, the user confirmed plainly: *"Okay it works, damn."* Clicking, typing, and focus all functioning normally — this closes out the ghost-overlay incident chain for tonight.

## Scopes & North Star

*Reference — the thesis-completion criteria and the generalization vision. The
actionable work lives in the [Task List](#task-list) (Scope #1/#2/#3 + the 8
capability dimensions); this is the* why.

### The three scopes *(thesis = all three)*
Chosen to span the interesting space — *data entry*, *cross-app transfer*,
*conditional judgment* — so the claim is "clones varied GUI workflows," not "fills
one form."

1. **Data Entry Form Filling** — *in progress ([Scope #1](#task-list)).* Single-app
   key-value entry, (mostly) linear. Loop proven on the Policy section (clones
   order, fills, submits); remaining = multi-tab, multi-record, cold-start.
   Perception = UIA.
2. **Web Form → Excel** — *not started ([Scope #2](#task-list)).* Cross-application
   transfer (web source → Excel grid); 2D target; mixed perception. Excel
   perception swap **PROVEN** (`ExcelObserver` normalizes to canonical); remaining
   = web source, action on cells, demos, train.
3. **Email / Ticket Triage** — *in progress ([Scope #3](#task-list)), Inbox
   Router approach.* Based on a passively-learned per-sender pattern profile
   (Gmail API + OAuth2, no manual labeling), an incoming email is routed to
   Scope #1's form-fill, routed to Scope #2's sheet-match, replied to
   directly, forwarded, flagged for a person, or left alone — then the
   outcome is confirmed by reply. Chosen approach (2026-08-16) doesn't need
   branching ([Big Three #3](#task-list)) at all, unlike the
   originally-planned GUI-demonstration path — decisions come from Gmail API
   classification, not a cloned UI action loop. Kept to decisions inferable
   from *visible* content (avoid hidden-intent, Fundamental roadblock A).

### North Star — generalization (beyond the thesis)
The thesis is *bounded* to those three, but the **architecture is built to
generalize** — that is the real goal. The novel contribution: a **personalized,
demonstration-learned GUI agent** that learns how *this user* does a task and
reproduces *their* workflow — which scripted RPA (no learning) and generic
computer-use agents (not personalized) don't.

Already partly real: perception is an **adapter** (UIA + Excel today, one shared
schema) and the `transformer(WHERE) + LLM(WHAT)` loop is perception-agnostic. The
path beyond the thesis — a vision perception adapter
([Big Three #1](#task-list)) + LLM-induced control-flow
([Big Three #3](#task-list)) — turns "three GUI scopes" into "any GUI
workflow learned from demonstration."

---

## Questions, Concerns, and Concepts

Open threads, honest unknowns/risks, and the **design concepts** (architectural
decisions + approaches like the WHERE/HOW division and DAgger) that shape how the
system evolves. **Questions** = unknowns to resolve · **Concerns** = risks to watch
· **Concepts** = decisions made + design patterns to build toward.

### Decisions & concepts

**WHAT does the transformer learn — Pure (A) vs Division-of-labor (B)? → CHOSE B.**
The 3-tab navigation marathon (2026-06-11) exposed that the transformer's
*action-type head* (deciding click-vs-type per step) is **unstable** — it whipsaws
between all-click (combobox/field spiral) and all-keyboard (tabs through, never
clicks) across retrains. Two ways forward:

- **Option A — Pure transformer.** The transformer learns *everything*, including
  click-vs-type, purely from demos, **zero rules.** Honest cost: a days-to-weeks
  data + training grind (more consistent + transition-dense demos, bigger model,
  stable recipe, likely DAgger), with real risk of a BC ceiling (long-horizon +
  rare events + compounding error is the textbook case where pure BC underperforms).
- **Option B — Division of labor *(CHOSEN)*.** The **transformer drives WHERE** —
  which element, what order, *when to switch tabs* (the learned, personalized
  navigation = the thesis claim, 100% intact). **Widget TYPE drives HOW** — type
  into an editcontrol, click a tabitem, open+select a combobox, toggle a checkbox
  (universal GUI mechanics, identical for every user/app — *not* form-specific).

**Why B:** click-vs-type is **not personalization** — it's universal plumbing, true
for every app. Forcing the unstable head to learn it blocks scope #1 for zero
thesis benefit. B keeps the personalization claim ("learns the user's navigation/
order/workflow") fully intact while freeing the one unstable decision onto rules
that **already exist in the agent** (combobox/checkbox handlers etc. are already
widget heuristics — the agent was never "pure"). Net: ~5 universal widget→action
rules, no form-specifics, navigation still 100% learned. Finishes scope #1 in
hours vs weeks. *(If a reviewer asks: the claim is "clones the user's workflow,"
not "learned how to physically touch every widget with zero rules.")*

### DAgger — how to implement *(if/when pure-BC drift needs it)*
The principled fix for the live drift seen on 2026-06-11 (agent leaves the form,
oscillates) = train on the states the agent ITSELF visits, not just clean demos.
Loop: train → run policy → label the correct action for visited (drift) states →
aggregate → retrain → repeat. Labeling is the only real choice:
- **Manual (closest to ready):** the `CorrectionHandler` seam already exists but
  **captures 0 steps (broken)** — fix it so after a failed/no-change step the agent
  pauses, the user performs the right action, and `(state, action)` is recorded.
  Targeted (only fix mistakes), far cheaper than re-recording passes.
- **Autonomous (no human):** demo-retrieval oracle — embed the visited state
  (`encode_state`), find the nearest demo state, copy its action as the label.
  Approximate but fully automatic; fits the "learn from demos, not manual" goal.
- **Blocker:** `CorrectionHandler` capturing 0 is *why DAgger isn't usable yet* —
  un-breaking it is the first concrete step. Needs a few run→correct→retrain rounds
  to converge.

### Open technical questions
- **Loss-weighting is less plug-and-play — how do we improve it in the future?**
  The 2026-06-12 rare-event fix (up-weight the rare action's frames in the loss so
  the model learns it without distorting the data — see Concepts) still requires
  *manually labelling which action class is rare* (here: clicks whose target is a
  `tabitem`). For a truly general system, that labelling should be **automatic.**
  Directions to explore:
  - **Auto-detect rare classes by frequency** — at dataset build, histogram the
    target action classes (per element-type / per action), then weight each
    **inversely to its frequency** (`w ∝ 1/freq`). Zero manual tagging; any rare
    action self-emphasizes. The general version of what we hand-built for tabs.
  - **Focal loss** — automatically down-weights easy/common examples and focuses
    gradient on hard/rare ones, *without* any class labels at all. The most
    plug-and-play; no frequency bookkeeping.
  - **Make rare actions CONDITIONAL (state feature)** — give the model a signal
    that predicts the rare action (e.g. "section-complete fraction"), so rarity
    stops mattering. More robust but needs the trigger identified per action.
  → Likely end-state: **inverse-frequency auto-weighting (or focal loss) as the
  universal default**, with optional state-features for robustness where a clear
  trigger exists. Reusable across every scope's rare actions.
- **Will full-form cloning scale on data alone?** Policy tab (~10 fields) needed
  ~20–30 passes. The full form is 176 fields / 8 tabs with rare **tab-transition**
  events. Unknown whether ~30–50 passes clone it, or whether long-trajectory BC
  needs far more demos than is feasible to hand-record. *(The `three_Tabs` probe
  is the cheap test of this.)*
- **Cold-start without hardcoding.** First click from a blank screen is ~0% and
  proven resistant to data + model size. Needs DAgger / a learned start-signal —
  but a deterministic anchor is off the table (no hardcoding). Open: how?
- **Is the >95% transformer-dependency target even right?** Stuck ~62–68%. By
  design the LLM owns *values*, so the transformer can't drive the value-typing
  steps. Maybe the honest ceiling is lower and the metric should exclude
  value-steps — needs a definition decision.
- **Excel action half is untested.** Perception swap is proven, but *acting* on
  cells (click → type → Enter, grid navigation) isn't. Will the form-specific
  executor handlers (combobox/checkbox) interfere on Excel? Probe before P1.
- **Cycle-restart loop root cause** — new record re-clicks the already-checked
  Renewal checkbox → Tab → no-change loop. Is it a per-record state-reset bug or a
  model issue? Decides whether it's an easy fix or needs data.

### Strategic / thesis concerns
- **Is perception-swap enough evidence, or must we show full Excel completion?**
  We proved Excel *perception* conforms (zero edits). The stronger claim — a model
  actually completing a task on a 2nd app — needs action + demos + training. How
  much does the thesis require?
- **Scope #3 (triage) is no longer stalled.** The originally-planned
  GUI-demonstration path is still genuinely blocked on Action-Space (#2) and
  Control-Flow (#3) — those remain parked in North Star. But the chosen
  Inbox Router approach (Gmail API + OAuth2, see Task List) doesn't depend
  on either one, so Scope #3 is actively moving without waiting for them.
- **Web perception may force the vision adapter early.** Scope #2's web source may
  lack a clean accessibility tree → could pull Big Three #1 (vision) forward of
  plan. De-risk: probe the web source's tree before committing.

### Risks / debt
- **Slow iteration loop** — every model test = record → retrain → live run.
  Velocity risk for the P0 reliability work.
- **Is the DAgger / correction loop actually working?** `CorrectionHandler`
  captures *0 steps* in live runs. Either nothing to correct, or it's not wired —
  verify before relying on it for cold-start.
- **Stale tests** — `test_transformer_bc / html_detector / two_state` fail to
  collect (`ModuleNotFound: components.intelligence.model.dataset`). Fix or delete.
- **Pixel-coordinate brittleness** — clicks use absolute screen px from the
  observer; sensitive to resolution / window position. Robustness concern at scale.

## Finished Tasks

Completed work, preserved for reference.

### Navigation cloning (2026-06)
- **Proved the transformer clones the demonstrated order** — top-down (74%) vs
  bottom-up (93%), same architecture → not a bias, genuine cloning.
- **`is_filled` perception feature** — model can see which fields are done →
  stopped the end-game looping.
- **Action-space collapse → {click, type}** — action-type accuracy 50% → 80%,
  click accuracy up for free.
- **Wired action-type into the agent** — transformer decides click vs type (was a
  hardcoded "fillable & empty" rule).
- **Recorder combobox fix** — clicks while a dropdown is open are value-selections
  (land on the field under the dropdown) → dropped at record time. Killed the
  phantom "Expiration Date" pollution.
- **Tail-oversampling** — emphasized the `… → Submit` finish → model learned to
  submit on its own (no hardcoded completion rule).
- **End-to-end run** — fills the whole form in the learned order + clicks Submit +
  advances record, transformer-driven, no crutch, no human.
- **Recorder focus fix** — clicks always get a fresh snapshot (was reusing stale
  state during bursts → focus stuck).
- **`scripts/clean_demos.py`, `test_clone.py`, `oversample_tails.py`,
  `replicate.py`** added.

### Intelligence & Training
- **GPU training** — CUDA 12.4 / PyTorch 2.6.0+cu124, RTX 4050.
- **Best-acc checkpoint** — saves on `val_acc + click_acc`, not val_loss.
- **Dataset init cache** — `.dataset_cache.pkl`, retrain init ~1 sec.
- **LayerNorm on pointer heads** — fixed bilinear Q×K divergence (197M loss bug).
- **Data augmentation** — `augment_traces.py` (bbox/click/confidence jitter).
- **Tasks/ reorganization** — model.pt, ruleset.md under `tasks/form_filling/`.

### Agent & Merge Logic
- **Crutch-gating in pure mode** — VISITED-ADVANCE + LLM-takeover-when-weak gated
  off under `disable_auto_handlers` (they fought the model).
- **Fix LLM click position** — `_merge` resolves LLM target by label; transformer
  click is fallback.

### Ruleset & Spec System
- **Correctional ruleset** — `RuleExtractor.correct()` + auto-call on record end.
- **Spec injection** — `ruleset.md` appended to LLM system prompt.

### Evaluation
- **Per-run metrics** — `eval_metrics.evaluate_run` (TCR, action/value accuracy).
- **BC fidelity scorer** — `bc_fidelity.py` vs gold standard, trend in
  `bc_progress.jsonl`.

### Infrastructure
- **Capsule registry** — per-task model routing.
- **`.gitignore`** — `data/demos/`, traces, caches, model binaries excluded.
